# dan-heng-permansor-terrae — Personal Memories

> Verbatim dialogue from the canon databank where this Heir appears, copied (never modified in the databank) to serve as personal memory. Each part shows the source file and the in-story context (chapter / mission / scene), and is bounded to a coherent dialogue moment.

---

### Part 1 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Navigation Meeting


> *(Ask)* Where's Sunday?
> **Welt:** He isn't joining us for the navigation meeting. He went to the Party Car to rest. Don't worry about him. An unexpected destination could be a great starting point for Sunday.
**Pom-Pom:** Since everyone's here, Pom-Pom will start presenting our options. First up is the oceanic planet of Lushaka.
**Dan Heng:** Mr. Mikhail's homeworld. Lushaka was hit by rising sea levels after the Stellaron Disaster.
**Pom-Pom:** Next, we have Melustanin, the Agate World.
**Himeko:** That's where Idrila the Beauty ascended, a world of undying allure. It's also said to be one of the initial sites of the Stellaron Disaster.

---

### Part 2 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Navigation Meeting


**Pom-Pom:** And finally, the most adventurous option: The Eternal Land, Amphoreus.
**March 7th:** A planet not recorded in the data bank and a world even Akivili has never set foot on... But if this trailblazing expedition works out, it could solve our fuel problem!
> *(Trailblazer)* The Express can soon achieve perpetual motion?
> **Dan Heng:** It's not that simple. But we can save Pom-Pom from worrying about service delays.
> *(Trailblazer)* We don't really have a choice in a time like this, do we?

---

### Part 3 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Navigation Meeting


**Welt:** I feel the same. While we know nothing about Amphoreus, we know the other two worlds aren't exactly safe options. Anyway, if it's a risk we're taking, we might as well throw in some adventure.
**Dan Heng:** Agreed.
**Himeko:** So, everyone's leaning toward the uncharted world? It seems we've got a crew of like-minded adventurers on the Express. That includes me, too. I'm for Amphoreus.
**Pom-Pom:** Looks like Pom-Pom's voting plan won't be needed! It's official, then. Our next stop is... Amphoreus!

---

### Part 4 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Chat with Companions (Optional)


**(Talk to Welt):**
**Welt:** A world yet to be explored by the Trailblaze... No self-respecting Nameless would turn that opportunity down.
> *An opportunity hard to come by?*
> **Welt:** Absolutely. The Astral Express has always traveled along the silver rail laid down by Akivili. We don't normally get to venture into a world beyond the knowledge of the Trailblaze. But this time, we might just get the chance to add a brand-new stop to the astral charts. I'm a little excited.
**(Talk to Dan Heng):**
**Dan Heng:** I can't provide many details about our destination this time. You can ask the Memokeeper if you want to know more.
> *Are you nervous, Dan Heng?*
> **Dan Heng:** Why do you ask that? Are you feeling nervous yourself? It'll be fine. Just trust the Crew.
> *Oh ho, the data bank's getting a huge update.*
> **Dan Heng:** Mhm, I suppose there's a lot to do. I'll disembark with all of you this time. We've never had this many passengers on board before... I'm still not used to it.
**(Talk to March 7th):**
**March 7th:** Hey, great timing! I just turned on my camera and was looking through some old photos. Can I ask you something? What's your favorite part of a journey?
> *The start, when we dive into the unknown.*
> **March 7th:** Then let's go snap some photos as soon as we arrive in Amphoreus!
> *The end, when we commemorate the adventure.*
> **March 7th:** Me too! This time let's make sure we get a group photo that everyone loves!

---

### Part 5 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > The Warp Jump and Amphoreus Revealed


**Himeko:** We already know one of the three Paths to be the Erudition...
**Black Swan:** I can reveal the second — the Remembrance.
**Welt:** I see. What about the final Path?
**Black Swan:** Unfortunately, fate is keeping the third Path a mystery. Hidden beneath the brilliance of Erudition and Remembrance, yet possessing enough power to rival the other two. It could be Equilibrium, Enigmata, or perhaps Permanence? This white ribbon of light encircling Amphoreus might very well be a product of the three interwoven Paths.
**Dan Heng:** Still, we know far too little about the planet. What's more pressing is finding a suitable landing site. We could end up over an ocean, in a vacuum without oxygen, or worse, in the middle of a lava field...
> *Now I see why the data bank's important.*
> **Dan Heng:** It is. Still, encountering worlds not recorded in the data bank is unavoidable.
> *Have no fear, resilient little me will volunteer!*
> **Dan Heng:** I know you're fearless even in the face of death, but don't act recklessly — everyone values you.
**Welt:** ...Wait, I think we might be missing someone.
> *Hey, where's March 7th?*
> **Welt:** Very funny... But seriously, where's March 7th?
**Dan Heng:** She said something about her camera before going back to her room. I haven't seen her since the warp.
**Himeko:** That's not like March. She's usually the most excited about expeditions. Let's drop by her room.

---

### Part 6 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Wake Up Dan Heng


> *Slap him in the face!*
> *(You suddenly felt the urge to "slap him" for no reason.)*
**(Trailblazer):** ...Sorry, Dan Heng.
**Dan Heng:** ...Sorry for what?
> *Oh, it's nothing.* / *When did you come to!?* / *Uh, pardon me for... not keeping you safe.*
**Dan Heng:** Ugh... How long were we out for? You passed out when our car got hit. I blacked out too, but not before dragging you out. It's a good thing we were in the car, or we would've been crushed to pieces. ...We should try making contact with the Express.
*(Message fails to send.)*
**Dan Heng:** I guessed as much... It's no longer working. Looks like it's just the two of us out here now. Let's find someplace safe to set up a base of operations first. This statue and the buildings we've seen so far point to a civilization here on Amphoreus... We can only hope they're not hostile toward outsiders.
> *It's dangerous here. We should leave.* / *Are we back to being fugitives again...?*
**??? (Mem):** Me...
**(Trailblazer):** ...?
**Dan Heng:** What is it?
> *I thought I heard something...* / *Shh. Someone's here.*
**Dan Heng:** ...Someone's here? Ready your weapon. Let's find out what's behind that gate.

---

### Part 7 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Investigate the Abnormal Noise


**Dan Heng:** Stay quiet.
*(Approach the gate)*
**Dan Heng:** Hold on— It opened on its own?
**(Trailblazer):** Why is it so dark? Is it nighttime already...?
*(The surroundings change)*
**(Trailblazer):** ...Wha?
**Dan Heng:** Over there. That pink thing.
**(Trailblazer):** You see it, too?
**Dan Heng:** Yes, it's not an illusion.
*(The surroundings change again)*
**Dan Heng:** There it is again. Is it doing all of this? This phenomenon... is hard to explain.
**Dan Heng:** It's gone... That happened too fast for us to make any sense of it. Did you see what it looked like?
> *A cat?* / *A rabbit?* / *The mastermind behind everything?*
**Dan Heng:** It's unlike any known species in our data bank. But I guess that's to be expected. We know nothing about Amphoreus. Back when we were trailblazing into Jarilo-VI, we knew its history and terrain even before arriving. But with Amphoreus... All we have is whatever little Black Swan told us.
> *But we can't afford to screw up.* / *What should we do now? Find a local?*
**Dan Heng:** I'm afraid we must plan for the worst. Putting a potential language barrier aside, that was a powerful attack on us just now... It's hard to imagine a planet as isolated as this having the expertise to create such sophisticated weaponry.
> *Is it any match for my baseball bat?*
**Dan Heng:** I can't tell for sure. But let's not get our hopes up too high.

---

### Part 8 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Explore Amphoreus — Photos for March


**(Trailblazer):** Hmm, now that's one monumental city gate.
**Dan Heng:** Imposing and somewhat archaic... Could it be a cultural site?
**Dan Heng:** Look at that.
> *What is that?* / *A giant sphere...*
**Dan Heng:** Judging from the prevalence of these colossal structures, this civilization has more than just great engineers. The structures also indicate a universal belief system. ...If you want to take some photos, now would be the perfect time.
*(Take photo: A giant sphere — Kephale's Corpus — stands between heaven and earth. At its top, the sky appears torn open, a swirling vortex churning within, clouds reflecting a golden glow.)*
**Dan Heng:** Don't forget the city gate, too.
*(Take photo: Across the cliff, grand palace gates remain silent, unwilling to reveal history and secrets hidden behind them.)*

---

### Part 9 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Explore Amphoreus — Photos for March


**(Trailblazer):** (These turned out great. They deserve the most prominent spot on the photo wall.)
**Dan Heng:** ...Hmm? Something's wrong... Watch out, (Trailblazer)!
> *Were they at this location before?*
**Dan Heng:** These things aren't breathing. ...Inorganic lifeforms? Looks like they want to keep us here. With a dead end and a cliff edge behind us, there's no other way out. We're going to have to fight them.
> *You can't say it's trailblazing until... you land a hit with your bat!*
**Dan Heng:** Let's do this.
*(Battle against Furiae Warriors, Archers, and Troupe — 3 waves)*
**Dan Heng:** They just keep on coming, how do we break free from this...?

---

### Part 10 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Meeting Phainon and Tribbie


*(Cutscene: A young person with white hair came down from above, defeating all monsters. He grabbed your bat and deflected — and broke — Dan Heng's spear.)*
**Dan Heng:** Look out—!
**??? (Phainon):** You've got something... interesting.

---

### Part 11 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Meeting Phainon and Tribbie


**Phainon:** I bear no harm. I just want everyone to be safe.
> *Everyone to be safe?* / *Hey, I can understand what he's saying...?*
**Dan Heng:** Everyone to be safe?
**Phainon:** Roaming around a place like the Abyss with weapons in tow is a show of provocation. Those soldiers are minions of Nikador, the Strife Titan. They're not known to stop until all invaders fall under their feet. If you continue brandishing your weapons, you won't only be putting yourselves in the crossfire... Their looming blades will come down on the heads of the innocent.
**Dan Heng:** A civil approach gets your message across all the same.
**Phainon:** It's difficult to tell if you're friend or foe when you appear out of thin air. Even without your weapons, a great power still lurks within you... I could be putting myself in harm's way for all I know. So, care to share the reason for dropping in on us? Hmm? Visitors from above?

---

### Part 12 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Meeting Phainon and Tribbie


**Phainon:** Alright, if you say so, Tribbie... I apologize for my earlier behavior. I was overly wary because both of you landed in this tumultuous region.
**Dan Heng:** I understand. We're Trailblazers from beyond the sky.
> *You're telling them everything already?*
**Dan Heng:** There is no point concealing anything. They saw everything. If they wanted us dead, they wouldn't be standing around talking to us.
**Phainon:** ...Not from above the sky but beyond it...?
**Tribbie:** Wow, that's something else altogether... You're lucky you met us, then.
**Dan Heng:** What do you mean?
**Phainon:** She means you're lucky because we won't hurt you, but the same can't be said for others.
**Phainon:** ...Let's talk somewhere else. There are still many refugees who haven't been freed in the temple. We're here to rescue and escort them to the holy city, Okhema. They're the reason I had to be absolutely sure you were not here with ill intent. I'm Phainon from Aedes Elysiae. Sorry if I offended you just now.
> *That's an unusual name.* / *Meh, I preferred it when you were being combative.*
**Dan Heng:** ...You sound disappointed. I'm Dan Heng. This is—
> *I'm (Trailblazer).*
> **Phainon:** Friction really does spark friendships, huh? Here, you can have your weapon back.
> *I'm the Galactic Baseballer.*
> **Phainon:** That sounds mighty impressive, but shouldn't you tell me your actual name?
>> *I'm (Trailblazer).*
>> **Phainon:** Friction really does spark friendships, huh?
> *Does no one else think we look like brothers?*
> **Phainon:** Well how about that? Perhaps it's fate! Friction really does spark friendships, huh?
**Tribbie:** And here's your spear. Don't worry, this is entirely on Snowy. I'll make sure he fixes it for you!
**Phainon:** Ahaha... It seems I'll have to treat Chartonus to a couple of drinks again.
**Dan Heng:** We've just arrived and still have many questions. Please lead the way.

---

### Part 13 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Private Discussion


**Dan Heng:** How do you view the situation?
> *There's more to this Phainon than meets the eye.*
> **Dan Heng:** I agree. To disarm you in a flash and even snap my spear... He's no ordinary person. He was also quite cautious, keeping his distance throughout. I tried probing for more information, but they both casually glossed over it. If things take a turn for the worse, our best bet is still going to be resolution by force.
> *Their appearance was suspiciously well-timed.*
> **Dan Heng:** Yes. The spear that destroyed our car is definitely man-made. I suspected they might have staged the encounter, but these refugees... they're clearly not pretending. For now, it's reasonable to assume they're not on the same side as the attackers.
> *How is it even remotely possible that we share a common language?*
> **Dan Heng:** I've been trying to come up with possible theories. Perhaps like Jarilo-VI, Amphoreus was served by the silver rail eons ago? But the data bank would at least have some record... The way the girl uses "we" and "us" to refer to herself is unusual, too. But compared with our other suspicions, sharing a common language is the least of our concerns. Let's get moving.
> *About your spear...*
> **Dan Heng:** Though losing the spear is unfortunate, we gained some important insight: Amphoreus' weapon-forging skills are on par with the craftsmanship that forged the Cloud-Piercer. Looks like this world is nowhere nearly as primitive as we had first thought.

---

### Part 14 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > The Temple Dispute — Noldus and Virtus


> *Every place has their fair share of troubles.*
**Dan Heng:** The elder spoke of the "end times" and claimed that no place in Amphoreus is safe. What did he mean by that...?
**Virtus:** Excuse me, sir? Everyone knows about the prophecy. How is it that you—
**Phainon:** Virtus, right now we must focus on convincing Mr. Noldus to return. The Abyss is swarming with fierce enemies. My friends, I have a rather bold request. Might I trouble you to fetch Mr. Noldus? Tribbie will join you. She can also answer any questions you may have.
> *Shall we consider our options?*
> **Dan Heng:** Phainon interrupted me as I was trying to gather information from that refugee, and now he's saying the girl will answer our questions. His intent is obvious.
>> *He's hiding something from the refugees?*
> **Dan Heng:** Yes. I believe he thinks it's worse for others to find out we know nothing about this world. He wants to hide the fact that we're visitors from beyond the sky. There's a rift between us and the locals, so let's play along for now. We can also learn what Tribbie thinks when we're alone with her.
> *We'll take it from here.*
**Dan Heng:** Alright, we'll go.
**Phainon:** You two are truly honorable! I'll stay behind to guard Virtus and the rest. Go quickly and come back safe.

---

### Part 15 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Tribbie's Miracle — Oronyx's Prayer


**Tribbie:** I'm sure you two have a ton of questions, don't you? As for why we should speak in private — quiet places are better suited for communicating.
**Dan Heng:** But the road ahead is... cut off.
> *Are you going to carry and fly us across?* / *Dan Heng is finally going to be able to jump?*
**Dan Heng:** What are you talking about...
**Tribbie:** Heh. Judging by your reactions, you weren't lying about being newly arrived travelers. In that case, don't blink, or you'll miss the next part. Also, Snowy wouldn't dare make plans for us, it's more like the other way around! Alright! Silence, please. We are about to summon a miracle...

---

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Tribbie's Miracle — Oronyx's Prayer


**Tribbie:** ...Once again stir the ripples of past reverie!
**Dan Heng:** This is... unbelievable.
*(Achievement: "Walk Along This Pillar of Light")*

**Tribbie:** Just what I was afraid of. So much for a peaceful chat... The history lesson will have to wait, my friends. We must help Mr. Noldus!
*(Battle against Furiae enemies)*
**Tribbie:** You both have impressive fighting skills!
**Dan Heng:** It would be better if I still had my weapon.
**Tribbie:** Oh, I'm sorry...
*(After the battle)*

### Part 18 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > The Ballad of the Twelve Titans


**Phainon:** The dromas caravan isn't here yet, so let's all rest.
**Dan Heng:** Regrettably, our questions are still unanswered. Now that things have calmed down, perhaps you two can find the time to answer a few?
**Phainon:** Of course. Please come this way.

### Part 19 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > The Ballad of the Twelve Titans


**Phainon:** Please ask me anything you wish to know.
> *(Ask)* What is this "end times"?
> **Phainon:** Just as the word implies, it's the time of strife before the world meets its end.
> **Dan Heng:** Are you saying your civilization is coming to an end?
> **Phainon:** To be precise, it's the whole world that's coming to an end.
> *(Ask)* What are the Titans?
> **Phainon:** The ancient gods of Amphoreus, once worshiped by humanity, now stand as their foes.

---

### Part 20 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > The Ballad of the Twelve Titans


> *That's all the questions I have for now.*
**Dan Heng:** Your answers are too concise...
**Phainon:** I'm sorry. I guess I'm not good at telling stories.
**Tribbie:** Yet, a tedious and overly long explanation isn't ideal either. Let me think... Right! Virtus and the others are priests from Janusopolis. They should be adept at playing the lyre. How about playing a song for our two friends? There's a ballad that's been passed down in Amphoreus since ancient times...

---

### Part 21 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Ride the Dromas to Okhema


**Dan Heng:** What magnificent creatures.
**Phainon:** These large mild-mannered creatures are called dromas. They're humanity's most loyal companions. Dromas are very docile, even first-time riders can steer them easily. Here, give it a try.
**Dromas:** Urrhh... uuuhhhh—

### Part 22 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Okhema Under Attack!


*(Cutscene: You woke up to Dan Heng's call. Okhema was under attack.)*
**Dan Heng:** ...Hey, wake up! The city's under attack!
*(Battle)*
**(Trailblazer):** You call this safe!? It's not how Tribbie described it at all!
**Dan Heng:** These are the enemies we met in the Abyss. It seems this attack is also the work of the Strife Titan.
*(After the battle)*
**Attacked Citizen:** You both fought so bravely. Are you Chrysos Heirs from a foreign land? Please, I beg of you! Save Okhema! The mad king Nikador has returned!
**Dan Heng:** Stay calm and leave this to us. You must hurry and find shelter.
**Attacked Citizen:** Thank you, thank you... Your comrades are fighting inside the city. Fate will guide you to them.
**Dan Heng:** I expected the holy city to be a strong sanctuary, but things are less peaceful than I thought.
> *Do you still have your broken spear?*
> **Dan Heng:** They're just minions. My cloudhymn magic will be sufficient.
> *He thought we were Chrysos Heirs.*
> **Dan Heng:** "The Deliverers mentioned the prophecy"... It seems the people have a lot of faith in Phainon and the others. Our priority now is to find Phainon.

---

### Part 23 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Okhema Under Attack!


**Dan Heng:** How's the situation?
**Phainon:** I never expected Nikador to lead an army and launch a surprise attack on the holy city... They and Kephale have always been mortal enemies, but now they've gone mad like a vicious beast.
> *We took care of some enemies.*
> **Phainon:** You have my gratitude. Thankfully, Okhema is not completely unprepared. The prophecy had warned us about this.
**Tribbie:** Mr. Noldus and the others have gone to seek shelter. You should evacuate along with the citizens.
> *We can help.* / *It's safer by your side.*
**Dan Heng:** What (he/she) meant to say is: We can help.
**Phainon:** Very well, but please remain by my side. Their attacks do not discriminate between friend and foe.

---

### Part 24 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Aid Okhema


**Pious Citizen:** O Kephale, Throne of Worlds, please end your long silence and grant humanity protection...
**Dan Heng:** What are you doing? It's not safe here!
**Stubborn Citizen:** No, no, no, it's very safe here. Watch! No one will die. The heroes will protect the city!
**Pious Citizen:** The prophecy has already revealed that the light of Kephale will shine upon all...
**Phainon:** Leave them be. Just as Mr. Noldus reveres Janus, faith is all some people have left in a world teetering on the brink of collapse. To honor their faith is the sacred duty of the Chrysos Heirs— Minions of Strife, you shall not harm these people!

---

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > The Three Tribbies


**Trianne:** We are Trianne!
**Trinnon:** We are Trinnon!
**Tribbie:** We are Tribbie— Wait, no, now is not the time for introductions!
**Dan Heng:** ...The surprises never cease in Amphoreus.
**Trianne:** Snowy, De has been trading blows with the main forces for what feels like forever! Hurry up and assist him!

### Part 26 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Mydei — The Successor of Kremnos


**Mydei:** It is you who forget the second half of her words: "Protect the citizens and eliminate the invaders." Since you've never considered the pair over there to be "citizens"... They seem to fit the definition of "invaders."
**Dan Heng:** What do you mean?
**Mydei:** The three priests of Janus share the same appearance and mind, and their senses are interlinked. The Okhemans have long been aware of your presence. You trust him that much? I suggest you think twice.
**Phainon:** Provoking your allies at such a critical juncture. Whose side are you on?

### Part 27 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Mydei — The Successor of Kremnos


**Mydei:** You, what's your name?
> *My name is (Trailblazer).*
> **Mydei:** Is that not also strange?
> *I am the Galactic Baseballer!*
> **Mydei:** Is that not also strange?
> *Heh, it's a secret!*
> **Mydei:** Isn't that name... also strange?
**Dan Heng:** Is he your companion? You guys don't seem to be on friendly terms.
**Phainon:** Well, that's the Chrysos Heirs. Revered as heroes by the masses, but still imperfect mortals at their core.

### Part 28 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Castorice — The Shadow of Death


**Phainon:** ...What a mess. This is Marmoreal Market, the most important residential area. Pushing through by force would result in needless casualties... But now our backup is here.
**??? (Castorice):** Lord Phainon. And... the two guests. Welcome to Okhema.
**Dan Heng:** These enemies... aren't moving?
**Phainon:** When I heard your footsteps, I thought Death was already writing my final chapter... Miss Castorice.
**Castorice:** An abrupt ending to an epic that has barely begun might also amaze the people. But today, Lord Phainon... Okhema needs you.
**Phainon:** Well, if the "mortician" of the holy city says I'm needed, then it sounds like I'm gonna live through this mess.

### Part 29 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Follow Castorice


**Dan Heng:** Their hostility has ceased?
**Castorice:** In the face of death, Strife will also cause hesitation to arise. I... am the shadow of death.
*(If you try to get too close: The chilling aura fills you with unease. An overwhelming darkness engulfs you... You're teleported back.)*

### Part 30 — `databank/missions/chapter-01-heroic-saga.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 1 — Silver Chariot, Away to that Blackened Land > Face Nikador


**Phainon:** Marmoreal Palace, the Lance of Fury, Nikador... They're all there, in the distance.
> *What's that sound!?* / *Mommy, I'm scared...*
**Dan Heng:** What a horrifying battle cry. Is that a Titan?
**Phainon:** That battle cry once crushed all in its path. It knocked down foes and allies alike. My limbs trembled uncontrollably as my weapon clattered to the ground. I could hear nothing but the frenzied and shameful beating of my own heart... Fear — this is why the Strife Titan is revered. If a warrior can face this terror and still step forward, no trial thereafter can shake the weapon in their hand. You two, if you wish to retreat, this is your final chance.
> *It's fine. I've already died a number of times before.*
**Dan Heng:** We have no interest in the Titan rites. As Trailblazers, lending a helping hand to unfamiliar worlds is simply our duty.
**Phainon:** Trailblazing... What an interesting concept. Surely, on your worlds, there must also be a Titan which is revered by the masses? There's no need to further confirm your resolve. We shall soon cross swords with the embodiment of Strife. And yet, faced against this menacing threat, your resolve only gleams more intensely. Let's get going. In the era when Kephale had not yet fallen silent, none could stand as their equal. Today, we inherit the duty of the gods, safeguarding the inhabitants of Amphoreus.

*(narrative)* The murals record in simple and delicate carvings the stories of Kephale creating life and building Okhema.
*(narrative)* In the murals, Kephale bears the enormous Dawn Device on their back. Golden divine blood courses across their body, flowing to the ground from the left-hand side of their chest and spreading in all directions.
*(narrative)* At their feet, the humans bathed in the golden blood work together to create a great city. This city is Okhema, the origin of Amphoreus civilization.
*(Interact with the poet, optional)*
**Bard:** ...The strife of day and night birthed the all-knowing giant. Their feet measure the earth and their back carries the dawn and dusk.
**Bard:** With the night as their curtain, they dreamt until they awoke. Only then did they realize: My perfection was born for creation.
**Bard:** Gods, please hear my wish:
**Bard:** Craft my likeness with fresh earth, and grant my intellect with your fruits. Illuminate my emotions with the wings of a butterfly and forge my fate with golden blood.
**Bard:** The perfect gods created the very first humans, and the first humans created the first city.
**Bard:** Okhema! Standing at the foot of the Throne of Worlds, your golden blood can be traced back to the very beginning.
**Bard:** Though the black tide devours the ancient lands, the holy city will cross over to a new world...
*(Approach Phainon, optional)*
**Phainon:** Ma'am, this item of yours is new! Completely new! There's no doubt about it.
**Elderly Appraiser:** T—That's impossible! This is an heirloom that's been passed down by my ancestors... Where's Theodoros? Get him over here to take a look!
**Phainon:** Although I'm not as experienced at doing appraisals as he is, I'm very confident about this. If you don't trust me, you can take it elsewhere for a second opinion.
**Phainon:** Or, I can burn it for you? You'll be able to tell once you smell it!
**Elderly Appraiser:** N—No way! You can't burn my family heirloom!
**Dan Heng:** I never would've thought that Phainon had such a hobby...
**Dan Heng:** He seems to be dealing with someone difficult right now. Let's leave him to it.

> **Aglaea:** In ancient times, Phagousa, the Chalice of Plenty, concealed the beginning of the world amidst billowing waves.
> **Aglaea:** When the ancient Ocean Priests discovered that place, they used spirit water to build a barrier in order to prevent the site from being disturbed by external forces. However, they also left behind a passageway.
> **Aglaea:** That passageway is none other than the ceremonial basin in front of you. Clear yourselves of all distracting thoughts and wash your faces with the spirit water inside the basin. When you next open your eyes, you shall witness for yourselves...
> **Aglaea:** The Vortex of Genesis: A great sanctuary that houses the original divinity of the twelve Titans. It's also the place where the prophesized Miracle of Genesis will occur.
> *(Trailblazer)* I can't wait to see it...
> *(Trailblazer)* Is it really that amazing?
> **Aglaea:** For your first time, I shall personally guide you into the depths.
> **Aglaea:** Close your eyes and hold your breath. Sever all other sounds and let my whispers fill your ears.
> **Aglaea:** Take a handful of the pristine water, lift your palms in front of your chest, and feel the water gently flow away through your fingers...
> **Aglaea:** Sink deeper and do this once more. Submerge your wrists in the spirit water. Familiarize yourself with its temperature, its touch...
> **Aglaea:** Spread your fingers, then lower your head and search for the palms of your hands. Feel the spirit water flow against your face, until all of your senses are submerged...
> **Aglaea:** Floating, sinking...
> **Aglaea:** Diving into the depths.

**Aglaea:** This is forbidden territory which can never be ventured into.
> *(Trailblazer)* So you're saying that it's better to pretend it doesn't exist instead?

> **Aglaea:** Divine punishment has never been fair. As Okhema's guardian, I must do the same.
> **Aglaea:** This is a world dominated by Titans. And that order can only be toppled by the Chrysos Heirs.

### Part 113 — `databank/missions/chapter-02-light-slips.md`

**Dan Heng:** This has been bothering me for a while now. Everything we have seen thus far suggests that Amphoreus wasn't always isolated from the rest of the universe. Be it the birth of the Titans or the encroaching black tide, none of these can be explained as a "natural progression" of a civilization.

### Part 128 — `databank/missions/chapter-03-through-petals.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Speak with Dan Heng — The Citizens' Assembly


**Dan Heng:** Citizens' assembly?
**Phainon:** That's right. Oh (Trailblazer), you're back. Just in time. This is something that the both of you should probably know.
> *(Trailblazer)* Some good news?
> **Phainon:** Good news? I wouldn't call it that.
> *(Trailblazer)* More trouble?
> **Phainon:** I wouldn't call it trouble. I just think it will be favorable if the both of you understand the situation in Okhema better.
**Dan Heng:** Do you remember the Council of Elders that was on our heels previously? It seems like they've made their move.
**Phainon:** Some background information for you: The Council of Elders is an authoritative body in Amphoreus made up of different city representatives. They hold legislative power over the holy city and are in charge of presiding over the citizens' assembly held once every few years. This is the most important social event of Okhema. It is highly influential... and can even affect what happens to the Flame-Chase Journey.
> *(Trailblazer)* So Aglaea doesn't actually call the shots?
> **Phainon:** Despite her vision and wisdom that spans across thousands of years, even she cannot win over the hearts of everyone.
> *(Trailblazer)* I have never once passed my sociology class.
> **Dan Heng:** ...Perhaps you should retake some exams next time we visit Paperfold University.
**Phainon:** Every five to ten years, the Council of Elders organizes a citizens' assembly at Dawncloud. During the assembly, the representatives will vote on major issues such as whether the Chrysos Heirs led by Aglaea should still hold the right to govern the holy city and... whether the Titan's conquest should continue.
**Dan Heng:** It sounds like some sort of system to balance the heroes and the mortals.
**Phainon:** That is correct. However, the problem lies in the fact that there are ill-intentioned extremists in the Council of Elders. They view the citizens' assembly as a way to restrain the Chrysos Heirs and hope to take complete control over the Flame-Chase Journey. We can refer to them as the opposition. During the assemblies held in the past hundred years, the voices supporting the opposition have been growing stronger in the shadows. It's only thanks to Aglaea that the Flame-Chase Journey has been able to continue to this day. The citizens' assembly is just around the corner, but this time, there seems to be a new variable to the situation...
> *(Trailblazer)* Is it us?
> **Dan Heng:** No. Although we might be mentioned, the Council of Elders has another target in mind.
> *(Trailblazer)* Is it Mem?
> **Dan Heng:** ...An interesting train of thought, but the Council of Elders has another target in mind.
> *(Trailblazer)* Is it Anaxa?
> **Dan Heng:** It looks like you've realized it as well.
**Phainon:** I'm afraid that the Council of Elders might want to take advantage of Professor Anaxa. Aglaea will surely take back Cerces' Coreflame. But with Anaxa's current condition... It's so unique that nobody knows how taking the Coreflame away from him will affect him. He has always been someone that is only concerned about his own research. I'm certain that he must want to escape from the monitoring of the golden thread and protect himself. If the opposition were to offer an olive branch at this time...
**Dan Heng:** You suspect that he might betray the Flame-Chase Journey and defect toward the Council of Elders?
**Phainon:** Anaxa has never believed in the prophecy, so technically it cannot be considered a betrayal. However, he has taught many Chrysos Heirs and has long been considered an important ally of Okhema. I truly do not wish to... see comrades turning on one another.
**Dan Heng:** I have a better understanding of the full picture now. Is there anything that we can do?
**Phainon:** As I believe in his character, I plan to plead with Aglaea and try to buy more time for him. Then, the greatest way in which you can help is for you to know what I am fighting for.
**Dan Heng:** We understand. Thank you for being honest with us. Things are piling up again... (Trailblazer) and Mem are tied up with Oronyx's trial, and I'm preparing to visit the Grove to uncover clues about the world beyond the sky.
> *(Trailblazer)* Let's just take things one step at a time. / *I just want to rest right now.*

### Part 132 — `databank/missions/chapter-03-through-petals.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Wake Up in the Private Bath Chamber


**Dan Heng:** I'll keep it simple — you succeeded. Oronyx has entrusted you with their divinity. Now, you're no different from a demigod of Amphoreus. However... ...Mem has told me about what happened during the trial. They are still explaining things to the others and told me to bring you back to your room to rest first.
> *(Trailblazer)* The Titan must be joking, right...? / *Don't sigh, Dan Heng...* / *So... what's my superpower?*
**Dan Heng:** ...For you to be able to say that even now... You're truly impressive. There's something that... I've never told you. When we landed on Amphoreus, our car was pierced by Nikador's lance. Do you remember? Back in the ruins of the Abyss, this was what I said: "You passed out when our car got hit. I blacked out too, but not before dragging you out."
**Dan Heng:** Actually... My account kind of made light of the situation. What actually happened was much more complicated. You were severely injured back then, with several wounds still bleeding profusely. I struggled to carry you out and used the last of my strength to stem the bleeding. But then, I was also hit by a falling rock and lost my consciousness. The last time I checked, you still weren't breathing. But when I regained consciousness... I saw that you were alive and full of energy, so I never brought this up again.
> *(Trailblazer)* How exactly did I come back to life?
> *(Trailblazer)* Haha, so you were knocked unconscious by a rock!
> **Dan Heng:** ...That's beside the point.
**Dan Heng:** I'm not sure whether the gaze of Fuli has anything to do with this matter, but according to the Titan, you are living in the form of a memory right now, just like those objects of the past in the miracles of Oronyx. Perhaps Mem is the anchor rope that ties you to the present. I had assumed they couldn't stray too far from you, but the truth... might be the exact opposite.
> *(Trailblazer)* What should we do next? / *Cry, cry for me.* / *Stellaron, do something!*
> **Dan Heng:** For a while, I also thought that the Stellaron was your life support system, but it seems like... this hypothesis has been disproven.
**Dan Heng:** It's not time for us to despair yet. There must be a way to save you. Oronyx mentioned "finding your stolen future" and "cheating death." Considering Mydei's indestructible body, it seems to suggest that there is some kind of miraculous power in Amphoreus that can even reverse death... The key seems to lie with the Hand of Shadow, whose whereabouts are unknown.
**Mem:** (Trailblazer)... Are you alright? I heard you two talking about Thanatos. Cas is also very concerned about the warning. She has left the Vortex and returned to the garden. Shall we go and look for her? Thanatos is the Titan that Cas has always been looking for. Regardless of whether there are any clues, there's probably no one else that understands Death better than her.
**Dan Heng:** Let's do that then, (Trailblazer).

**Dan Heng:** ...

**Dan Heng:** ...Merely something from the past that I won't go in to.

**Hyacine:** I stood at this very spot back then, imagining the day when I could climb to the top of this tree... Because I would be the closest I can be to the sky at that spot.
**Dan Heng:** You're a Sky Priest, yet you study at a place that is home to the Reason Titan... That's rather counterintuitive.
**Hyacine:** Don't read too much into it. I mean, can mortals even hope to reach Aquila's realm? They're a strict and draconian god, nothing like Cerces. Reason is willing to share what is theirs with others, but Sky... only expects people to revere them.
**Hyacine:** You get what I'm saying, right Dannie? After all, the Titan that imprisoned you and (Trailblazer) here in Amphoreus is none other than Aquila themself...
**Dan Heng:** Yes, I didn't count on provoking the ire of a local god.

**Hyacine:** It's just... I never thought Grayie would meet with something like that before we set off... As a physician, I should have been there by her side.
**Dan Heng:** Bad luck tends to come in streaks, so don't beat yourself up over it. Besides, Aglaea needs you here to uncover the truth behind what happened to the Grove. Let's just make the most out of this trip and keep a lookout for any documents related to the Death Titan — they could be useful to (Trailblazer).
**Hyacine:** Yeah... There's no rest for the weary, huh.
**Hyacine:** In any case, what we're looking for could be anywhere in this enormous Grove. Let's split up to cover more ground. Dannie, with your skills, I'm sure those monsters born of the black tide will pose no threat to you anyway.

**Hyacine:** Hehe, have you forgotten that you're talking to the chief physician of the Twilight Courtyard? Have more faith in me. I'll be just fine!
**Dan Heng:** ...The silence in here is near deafening.
**Dan Heng:** With the vast amount of knowledge it holds, this academy is akin to a labyrinth of information... Even the cleverest of scholars would easily lose their way in here. I should gather my thoughts before heading in.
> *(Trailblazer)* How to leave Amphoreus...

> **Dan Heng:** (Hyacine's forebears were priests who worshiped Aquila. Nobody is more familiar with the taboos of the Sky Titan than her. Seeing how Aglaea has sent her here as a guide, it's possible that she plans to have Hyacine take on a trial in the future.)
> *(Trailblazer)* Amphoreus' Titan mythology...
> **Dan Heng:** (In Amphoreus, Titans are a common topic of discussion. People create myths that, in turn, conceive Titans... It's a tricky task to keep an objective view when among the people of Amphoreus.)
> **Dan Heng:** (Is there a way to step out of the situation and look at it from a different perspective?)
> *(Trailblazer)* Titans and Aeons...
> **Dan Heng:** (Titans and Aeons are two distinct classes of beings. Titans can communicate with us humans, and it is not impossible for us to overpower them. Upon their demise, we can even take over their divinity.)
> **Dan Heng:** (In this regard, Titans can be said to be a lot more similar to humans than Aeons are...)
> *(Trailblazer)* The Chrysos Heirs and demigods...
> **Dan Heng:** (Chrysos Heirs are humans who possess heroic qualities, with most of them demonstrating extraordinary combat prowess and strength. Upon clearing a Coreflame trial, a Chrysos Heir can even ascend to a demigod and inherit a Titan's divinity...)
> **Dan Heng:** (Wait, something about that feels off. Is this a... selection process of some sort?)
> *(Trailblazer)* The Flame-Chase Journey and the prophecy...
> **Dan Heng:** (Guided by the prophecy, the heroes are on a quest to reclaim Coreflames and usher in the Era Nova. Their efforts have seen gratifying results to date.)
> **Dan Heng:** (As I expected, the prophecy is the hardest to make any sense of. But with barely any information to go on, no matter how much I ruminate on it, it won't yield any results... I should leave this for another time.)
> *(Trailblazer)* Think of something else...
> **Dan Heng:** (I should probably turn my attention to something else.)
> *(Return to the previous options)*
> *(Trailblazer)* (Trailblazer)'s plight...
> **Dan Heng:** (Nobody could've ever imagined that (Trailblazer)'s life would be forfeited the moment we landed on Amphoreus...)
> **Dan Heng:** (I should have done more to keep her safe... but it's not too late. We still have a fighting chance — the unique rules that govern life and death on Amphoreus might just work in our favor and save her.)
> **Dan Heng:** (The Grove is a veritable library of knowledge... Maybe they'll have records on a similar case? Please let there be one...)
> *(Trailblazer)* Enough musing for now. It's time to move.
> **Dan Heng:** The clock is ticking... I should search for clues as I move.

### Part 134 — `databank/missions/chapter-03-through-petals.md`

### Part 138 — `databank/missions/chapter-03-through-petals.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Sub-Mission 3: Papyrus, Read the Blasphemer's Will > Speak with Hyacine


**Dan Heng:** Anaxa... goras. He's the scholar who fused himself with the Coreflame of Reason.
**Hyacine:** This is new. I didn't know Professor Anaxa was researching the Sky. He's never mentioned anything about it.
**Dan Heng:** Phainon said Anaxa might be opposed to the Flame-Chase Journey.
**Hyacine:** "Might" is an understatement. Anaxa has never been one to venerate the gods, nor has he ever found it within himself to put any faith in the prophecy. People have come to think of him as a heretic, even in a place as liberal as the Grove.
**Hyacine:** Yet, it is also this very "blasphemer" that Cerces favors. Fate sure works in mysterious ways.
**Dan Heng:** I picked up a scroll on my way here, too. It looks like it may be related to the one you're holding.
**Hyacine:** Oh? Let me have a look.
*(Hyacine combines both records together...)*
**Hyacine:** By the looks of them, these scrolls are records of two conversations that took place in the same setting, one after the other.
**Dan Heng:** There's still much I don't know about the Grove. Do you mind if I ask you a few questions?
**Hyacine:** Sure, go ahead.
> *(Trailblazer)* Who are the Venerationists?
> **Hyacine:** The Grove of Epiphany is home to seven schools of thought. While all seven schools are founded on the ideologies of Thalesus the First Scholar, each is unique in its own way. Among them, the Venerationists revere and worship the Titans the most.
> **Hyacine:** As the sage of the Venerationists, Euthyphro has always been at loggerheads with Anaxa.
> **Hyacine:** If we were to place the Seven Sages on the scales of Talanton, Anaxa and Euthyphro would be placed on opposite sides to achieve balance.
> **Dan Heng:** A very illustrative explanation. Thank you.
> *(Trailblazer)* Was it a mistake to build the skyships?
> **Hyacine:** Nearly everyone in Amphoreus has heard the story of the skyships by now. Aquila's stature is loftier than the heavens, and their divine retribution is merciless.
> **Hyacine:** That's why it's no use building giant sky-faring ships... Oh, but I do wonder if a supercharged mini Tribbie cannon could slip past their watch?
> **Dan Heng:** That was a joke... right?
> *(Trailblazer)* What is Anaxa's field of study?
> **Hyacine:** Nousporists primarily study the application of alchemy in soul repair.
> **Hyacine:** Everyone considers Anaxa a blasphemer, but that has never stopped him from sharing his ideas, no matter how taboo. If you must say that he's hiding something... then it's likely what lies beneath his eye patch.
> *(Trailblazer)* I don't have any more questions.
> **Hyacine:** Anaxa often says that... "Every failure brings us one step closer to the truth."
> **Hyacine:** Perhaps, to him, the crash of the skyship carries a different interpretation. The firmament is not impenetrable. On the contrary, it was precisely because the skyship represented the first step toward breaking free from the shackles of the earth that it provoked the wrath of the Sky Titan and brought divine punishment upon itself.
> **Hyacine:** It's a shame that the majority of the Grove's research on Aquila was headed by the Venerationists, with sacrificial rituals making up a predominant part of their findings. Discourse about more esoteric subject matters in relation to Aquila dwindled even further after the skyship incident, so much so that even this scroll is completely new to me.
> **Dan Heng:** Don't worry about it. We'll just collect everything we can find on the Titans for now.
> **Dan Heng:** There's something else that's caught my attention, though... This says that Anaxa is conducting secret experiments of some sort?
> **Hyacine:** That's what I'd like to know. What are these experiments he's involved in? Even I, the assistant lecturer, know next to nothing about them...

### Part 139 — `databank/missions/chapter-03-through-petals.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Sub-Mission 3: Papyrus, Read the Blasphemer's Will > Head to the Library of Philia


**Hyacine:** I have a bad feeling about this. Could there be more to what happened at the Grove?
*(Approach the marked location)*
**Hyacine:** This is the Library of Philia, a place where the Grove stores its collection of tomes.
**Hyacine:** Other than books written by the seven schools, you can also find philosophical texts, ballads, and religious writings from the outlands here. Cities that desired to learn from the Grove were apparently expected to contribute knowledge of equal value to what they sought.
**Hyacine:** We've had more than our fair share of hilarious episodes over the years. For instance, it's said that the Erythrokeramists once borrowed a manuscript from a certain author but ended up returning a copy instead of the original! What's more, the original manuscript was supposedly then hidden here.
**Dan Heng:** That... doesn't seem like a nice thing to do.
**Hyacine:** Nice? No need to hold yourself back, Dannie. You can just tell it like it is: Their behavior was no different from bandits!
**Dan Heng:** Well, if you don't mind: Did the Grove ever rob Castrum Kremnos?
**Hyacine:** You've got me there. Some said you couldn't even find a dictionary in Castrum Kremnos, so I guess they were spared.
**Hyacine:** There are too many books in the Library of Philia. Flipping through them all will take too long. Why don't we use the biblioslate to search for the texts we need? We can bring them back to Okhema for reading after that.
**Dan Heng:** Agreed, this place isn't safe. We should leave as soon as we're done.
**Dan Heng:** There are still several more scrolls strewn all over the place. I'll gather them while you pull up those texts, this way, nothing will be overlooked.

> *(narrative)* You skim through it, and the highlighted methods include virtual causation, false premises, contradiction conversion, conflating prevarication and reality, misinterpreted punctuation, misattribution, and generalizing specificity, among others.
> *(narrative)* Amidst it are scholars' exclamations: "A true eye-opener, the art of language knows no bounds," "If I can't master sophistry, I can at least learn to defend myself against them as an honest man," and so on.

**Dan Heng:** ...This isn't going to be of any help, that's for sure.

> *(narrative)* A record of the many research topics a Caprist scholar had applied for, and the replies from their school's Sage about these topics...
> *(narrative)* Topic 1: A discovery that many mythical beasts renowned for their longevity actually reside in areas with poorly managed birth and death records for such creatures. Permission to research. Reply: Lacks research significance, not approved.
> *(narrative)* Topic 2: An observation of real plants within the Grove imitating the growth patterns of nearby White Dryads. Permission to research. Reply: Falls under the domain of the Lotophagist school, not approved.

### Part 141 — `databank/missions/chapter-03-through-petals.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Sub-Mission 3: Papyrus, Read the Blasphemer's Will > Search for the Materials Left by Anaxa


**Hyacine:** Ack, it's so dusty in here... Whoa, are these all complaints about him?
**Hyacine:** I can already see what they must have written: "Anaxa has no respect for the gods! His very existence undermines order in our society!" ...They're probably all things like that.
**Dan Heng:** Does Anaxa hold that much influence?
**Hyacine:** Why else would they call him "the Great Performer"? When Anaxa established his school of thought, the first thing he apparently did was read his own work to everyone.
**Hyacine:** "Are the Titans truly an accurate reflection of what humans think gods should be? Worlds created by gods are indubitably perfect, but what of Amphoreus? Only foolishness and insensitivity everywhere you turn!"
**Dan Heng:** ...An uncanny impersonation. What happened after?
**Hyacine:** They marched him straight to the seat of judgment before he could finish.
**Hyacine:** Though what he took on in the end was more a debate than an actual trial. He had to address every one of the scholars' allegations, or face expulsion from the Grove.
**Hyacine:** The first allegation was... ahem. "Do you agree that since the Titans created the heavens and earth, so the mountains, rivers, and seas must likewise be their creations?"
**Dan Heng:** ...Am I supposed to put myself in Anaxa's shoes and answer that?
> *(Trailblazer)* Yes.
> **Dan Heng:** As told in the myths, the seas, the skies, and the earth are all, beyond the shadow of a doubt, creations of the Titans.
> **Hyacine:** Anaxa would've probably been spared a lot of trouble if he had answered this way... Instead, he quoted past instances of the Flame-Chase Journey and posited that humans have the potential to take over the divinity of Titans.
> *(Trailblazer)* No.
> **Dan Heng:** Based on the history of the Flame-Chase Journey, humans have the potential to take over the divinity of Titans.
> **Hyacine:** Anaxa said the same thing then.
> **Hyacine:** Needless to say, his critics didn't take kindly to his answer, so they followed up with a second allegation.
> **Hyacine:** "You claim to be studying souls, yet you are, in fact, defiling them and committing acts condemned by the gods. Do you admit it?"
> **Dan Heng:** That's a serious allegation. Did he really do that?
> **Hyacine:** Anaxa always wears an eye patch, never removing it, even when alone. Some say he used one of his eyes as an alchemical ingredient.
> **Hyacine:** Every being in the world owes their safekeeping to Kephale, and this is even more so for the Chrysos Heirs who have divine blood coursing through them. If he has really done something of that nature, then it'd indeed be sacrilegious.
> **Hyacine:** Thankfully, they had no way to prove it, so it amounted to nothing more than an allegation in the end.
> **Hyacine:** One by one, Anaxa engaged in wars of words against those who would challenge him. Toward the end, a scholar asked him, "Anaxagoras, have you any respect for the gods?"
> **Hyacine:** It was only after a brief silence that he answered, "I have never denied that Titans are meant to be noble constructs."
> **Dan Heng:** What he didn't say — that the Titans are not noble — rings louder in this case.
> **Hyacine:** Yes... I'm not sure how that controversy was eventually resolved, but what's for certain at least is that Anaxa was allowed to stay in the Grove where he continued his research, despite being regarded as a heretic.
> **Hyacine:** By the way, this stack of complaint letters has reminded me of a place in the Grove... We may find clues there that aren't on the teleslate.
> **Hyacine:** I'm talking about Anaxa's alchemy lab, hidden between the cornerstones.
> **Dan Heng:** Given the Grove's meticulous approach to archiving, could there really be such an oversight right under their noses?
> **Hyacine:** It's a well-kept secret. Among the Seven Sages, excluding the nominal Chief Sage — each has a private sanctuary where they can conduct research undisturbed in their free time.
> **Hyacine:** Even the administrators of the Library of Philia are restricted from accessing those places and documenting the sages' research. But...
> **Hyacine:** With the Grove in this state, I'm sure Anaxa won't be mad even if we sneak into the lab for a short while... Right?

Plus, considering that the Nousporists have crafted omnipotent medicines capable of curing diseases, it also makes sense that he would conduct research on immortal Chrysos Heirs.
> **Hyacine:** But now, it seems Anaxa's objective was never to create medicine but to identify the origin of the golden blood and figure out its inner workings.

### Part 144 — `databank/missions/chapter-03-through-petals.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Sub-Mission 3: Papyrus, Read the Blasphemer's Will > Search for the Materials Left by Anaxa


**Hyacine:** My biggest fear... It's come true... After going through his notes, I think I can piece it all together now:
**Hyacine:** Anaxa was carrying out experiments on soul transmutation.
**Dan Heng:** Do you mean he's trying to reconstruct human souls alchemically?
**Hyacine:** Yes, this is what he was secretly conducting research on — something he had to keep hidden from others. Compared to his public speeches, this is the real "blasphemy"...
**Hyacine:** Ah! I just thought of something...

**Hyacine:** That's right... but what if Professor Anaxa's fusion with the Coreflame of Reason wasn't an accident?
**Dan Heng:** What do you mean?
**Hyacine:** Put yourself in his shoes. Knowing that the black tide was closing in and your time was running out, what would you have done?
**Dan Heng:** ...
**Dan Heng:** ...I'd put my theories to the test, no matter the cost.
**Hyacine:** Exactly. **When the black tide came, the true reason Anaxa headed to the Luminary Throne was... to transmute and fuse himself with the Titan's Coreflame.**

**Hyacine:** The manuscripts detailing all the alchemical formulae have been taken away. His research... is still ongoing.
**Dan Heng:** Could he have taken them for other purposes?
**Hyacine:** Like what? There's only one Titan left in the world now—
**Hyacine:** ...
**Hyacine:** No... no way...
**Dan Heng:** "To conceive an entity of perfection, one must first understand the principles of creation"... If Anaxa is in Okhema now, then that means...

### Part 147 — `databank/missions/chapter-04-fall-at-dawn.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 2 — Scrolls, Turn the Blade's Gaze > Head to the "Abyss of Fate" Janusopolis — Dan Heng & Lygus


**Lygus:** ...So, this is the car of the Astral Express you've spoken of. My repository of memories has been enriched with another fascinating piece of information. Thank you for leading me here, Lord Dan Heng.
**Dan Heng:** I'm surprised a Council Elder like yourself would be willing to journey so far to inspect the Express.
**Lygus:** Okhemans hold wisdom and knowledge in high esteem. If the citizens knew of your origins, I'm sure more would be intrigued by "the Trailblaze." Sadly, discussions about the "world beyond the sky" remain taboo.
**Lygus:** It's been quite some time since you first arrived in Okhema. Presumably you've been looking for a way to leave ever since?
**Dan Heng:** I've been actively gathering clues and testing different things. Sadly, the more I find out, the more powerless I feel.
**Lygus:** Don't be too hard on yourself, my lord. This puzzle has stumped Amphoreus' scholars for centuries. It isn't something you can solve overnight... even someone as gifted as yourself.
**Dan Heng:** In the Grove, I discovered extensive research on the Sky Titan. It appears that many scholars from Amphoreus believe the taboo against the "world beyond the sky" stems from a curse cast by Aquila.
**Lygus:** Exactly. Of the Twelve Titans of Amphoreus, Aquila is known for being the most capricious. The weather itself reflects their mood: Tranquil sunshine, mournful rain. Aquila's breath is a brisk wind, and their cries resound as thunder in a storm.
**Dan Heng:** Additionally, Aquila is one of the two Titans whose Coreflames have yet to be returned.
**Lygus:** The sky above affects every citizen. It's clear that Lady Aglaea's decision to confront Aquila at the end of the Flame-Chase Journey was a carefully considered strategy.
**Dan Heng:** If returning Aquila's Coreflame can break the sky's curse, we won't have to exhaust ourselves searching for a way back. We can simply focus on aiding the Chrysos Heirs.
**Lygus:** Antikytherans have no home to return to, so I understand your concern for your old friends and homeland. I used Antikytheran technology — or "scanning," as you might call it — to assess this car. Assuming my judgment is correct, I might be able to conduct a thorough overhaul of its "power systems."
**Dan Heng:** Really? But...
**Lygus:** Of course, that's only if you, my lord, permit me to interact with it.
**Dan Heng:** ...By all means, please proceed. But be careful, the Express's thrusters are extremely delicate.
**Lygus:** You honor me with your trust. I will be very careful.
> *Lygus carefully scans the damaged propulsion system, then methodically began inspecting its components...*
**Dan Heng:** This... is unbelievable. You seem to understand its structure perfectly. Have you repaired something like this before?
**Lygus:** Not exactly. But we Antikytherans are inherently sensitive to machinery. Kephale endowed our kind with a unique advantage for analyzing and learning. If you give me some time, I might be able to fully restore this damaged car to its former glory. Then you and your esteemed companion could set off on your return journey sooner.
**Dan Heng:** But... isn't the real issue with Aquila?
**Lygus:** Hypothetically, Lord Dan Heng, if I could open up a path out of Amphoreus that circumvents Aquila's divine punishment... Would you consider ceasing your support for the Flame-Chase Journey?
**Dan Heng:** I don't understand... Does the Council of Elders possess some secret means of counteracting divine punishment?
**Lygus:** It's best if you don't delve too deeply into that question, my lord. All you need to know is, I can make it happen.
**Dan Heng:** (Maybe this is not a decision by the Council of Elders but rather his personal wish...)
**Lygus:** The crucial point is, if you and your companion are unwilling to halt your support for the Chrysos Heirs... it could offend certain interests.
**Dan Heng:** Hmm... Forgive me, Lord Lygus, but I must be frank. When visiting Dawncloud, I reviewed the records of many Council Elders... Among them, only your background was nearly blank. I'd like to accept your goodwill, but given such a mysterious background and motives, anyone cautious would have doubts.
**Lygus:** Your prudence impresses me, just as expected...
**Dan Heng:** Are these people— Is this a trap? Is that why you accompanied me here?
**Lygus:** Please don't misunderstand, Lord Dan Heng. As the Theoros, I have no allegiance to their plans nor am I obliged to obstruct them. I am neutral. Out of respect for you, I hope to offer a peaceful solution to ensure the safety of you and your companion.
**Dan Heng:** ...One against four, then. Just to confirm: if things turn ugly later... will you help?
**Lygus:** I will not. As I said, I remain neutral. Are you asking this because you plan to refuse my offer and continue supporting the Chrysos Heirs?
**Dan Heng:** You weren't wrong about one thing, Lord Lygus. Indeed, we must try to return to our companions... But our "companions" now also include the Flame-Chasing Chrysos Heirs. The Nameless cannot and will not ignore the fate of our companions.
**Lygus:** So I see. This is what you call "Trailblazing."
**Dan Heng:** When I asked if you'd help, I meant... the ones who need help are them.

### Part 148 — `databank/missions/chapter-04-fall-at-dawn.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 4 — Golden Thread, Relay the Savior's Fate > Go to the Marmoreal Palace


**Dan Heng:** These folks in black... Are not responding to provocations, nor do they seem intent on fighting. What exactly are they waiting for?
**Lygus:** Pressure. Fear. Planting seeds of anxiety. It seems this is the strategy they've chosen.
**Dan Heng:** An organization that sees it as their mission to eradicate the Chrysos Heirs... I've read some chilling accounts — stories of the "Cleaners" using alchemical arts to implant fragments of memories into their successors, ensuring both identity and hatred live on.
**Lygus:** From what I know, these stories are true. The Caenis we know today — strictly speaking — is the 27th Caenis. Objectively, the rise of the Cleaners was both inevitable and necessary. Yet, their ruthless and twisted methods earned them nothing but disdain and condemnation from history.
**Dan Heng:** And still, they endure. An organization persisting through time, now working tirelessly to obstruct the Flame-Chase.
**Lygus:** Even in these crumbling end times, there are always those who find ways to profit from the chaos. To ask the vested interests to abandon their status, wealth, and ambitions in this world for a distant ideal, may be no easier than creating a miracle.
**Dan Heng:** ...I wonder where (Trailblazer) is now. If they're targeting me, they definitely won't escape their notice either. I hope all is well... Is that... Lord Krateros?
**Krateros:** There you are, Lord Dan Heng. We couldn't reach you on the teleslate, so the Holy City Guard almost assumed you and (Trailblazer) had met some terrible fate. You pests crawling in the shadows, back off! Your plot ends here. You've played your hand. Run while you still can. It might be your only chance to survive. Stay away from Okhema forever. If you set foot there again, the fury of its people will tear you apart. **Because Aglaea is dead, and in the eyes of the holy city's citizens, her blood is on your hands!**

### Part 152 — `databank/missions/chapter-04-fall-at-dawn.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 5 — Grove, Judge the Past and Present > Third Ancestor — Grandmother Jacyntha (at Castrum Kremnos)


**Hyacine:** We're here. This is it.
**Dan Heng:** Castrum Kremnos...
**Hyacine:** Lord Mydei has been fighting the black tide here alone day after day... I'm worried about him.
**Dan Heng:** He sent a message earlier, carried by thunder, warning us to watch out for the Flame Reaver's actions.
**Hyacine:** There — that sleeping spirit... is my grandmother.
**Dan Heng:** Your... grandmother?
**Hyacine:** Yes. My grandmother Jacyntha was a warrior. She represented the Twilight Courtyard in the Kremnos Festival. She fought her way through every challenge and reached the final stage. She was just one step away from securing a peace treaty for the Skyfolk, but... was ultimately defeated by the champion warrior of that time. But behind the glorious duel... was a tragic end. Not long after the festival, my grandmother's injuries worsened, and she passed away far from home.
**Dan Heng:** And the Kremnoan warrior she fought against...
**Hyacine:** ...was Lord Krateros.
**Dan Heng:** ... Yet you still saved his life, even though he took your family.
**Hyacine:** Seeing him lying there, gravely wounded, bleeding out... I can't put what I felt at that moment into words. But when I heard Lord Krateros took that fatal blow to protect Lady Trianne... All those tangled feelings faded away. To quote Lord Phainon, Krateros must have done that "to become a better man."
**Dan Heng:** It takes true courage to forgive. You're every bit as strong as your ancestors, Hyacine.
**Hyacine:** Heh... You're getting better at this, Dannie. In her final diary entry, my grandmother wrote that when the moment of destiny comes, a descendant of the Sky people would deliver the message of their departure to her spirit. I wonder what she'd think... When the one awaking her turns out to be her beloved granddaughter.

**Dan Heng:** But we can't stay. (Trailblazer) and I... we have people waiting for us, a home calling us back.
**Hyacine:** The Astral Express, isn't it? March 7th, the one you always mention... I'd like to meet her someday. Of course, I do feel sadness. A sharp, aching sadness... But as a healer from the Twilight Courtyard, it's my duty to stay composed. A sorrowful expression would only add to their burden. The world I love is the one suffering now. How could I face it with a frown?

**Herta:** Or do you want to return to the audience's seat and give yourself, Amphoreus, and the entire cosmos a more fitting end?
**"Zandar":** I must refuse. A dead man never fears death.

**"Zandar":** ...

**"Zandar":** Listen, my peers. Why don't you look beyond this world, just as the Chrysos Heirs did? Re-evaluate your calculations, and take into account all enemies in the upcoming war among the Aeons.
**"Zandar":** Amphoreus is not a place where three Paths "entangle," but where three Paths "fight to the death."
**"Zandar":** You used memoria to resist me, but did you not consider the possibility that...
**"Zandar":** **The Remembrance and THEIR children might have taken this opportunity to join the fray as well?**

**???: ** ...

**???: ** ...

**???: ** How impulsive. That's not like you at all.

### Part 205 — `databank/missions/chapter-07-back-to-earth.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 6 — Earth, Bear the Suffering of All > Talk with the Mysterious Mountain Dweller


**???: ** And you, visitor from beyond the sky... You have no intention of listening to the cries of the "Earth."
**Dan Heng:** You are...?
**???: ** Are you surprised? Demigods with golden blood are not solely limited to Kephale's children. Is it not natural that the offspring of Georios would take on their duty?
**Terravox:** I am Terravox, avatar of the earth, divine protector of the myriad living beings.
**Dan Heng:** What a coincidence. I was just told by someone to watch out for you.
**Dan Heng:** Answer me: Why would the Dromas King of the legends, the demigod who should have fallen, appear in a humanoid form?
**Terravox:** You already know the answer: Is the Transmutation Arcanum not precisely your specialty, descendant of the Permanence?
**Dan Heng:** How do you know about...
**Dan Heng:** Was it "March 7th"?
**Terravox:** Exactly. Thanks to her, I was able to escape into the memoria tide and disappear from the world's memories.
**Dan Heng:** So you didn't die. Your "fall" was just a lie. You cast aside your duty as a Flame-Chaser and joined her. Why?
**Terravox:** To survive. For a dromas, life is no more than "existence"...
**Terravox:** The birds and beasts of this world have struggled to survive since time immemorial. Yet, unlike humans, they are unable to live on in the Worldbearer's memories.
**Terravox:** But Evernight is the master of Remembrance, and I saw your story in the Veil of Evernight—
**Ichor Memosprite:** █ ██ █Vidyadhara █ ██ the Permanence █ dragon transmutation ██ █ █resurrec █tion█ ████ █
**Dan Heng:** The path you chose to survive will lead to your death, demigod.
**Terravox:** And what of it? This body is soaked in filthy golden blood...
**Terravox:** The "Earth" is hungry, and only the Permanence can satisfy it!
*(Enter battle — Wave 1: Ichor Memosprite: Pheasant*2; Ichor Memosprite: Canine*2)*
**Dan Heng:** This madness seems exactly like a mara-struck.
*(Wave 2: Cloud Knights Patroller*3)*
**Dan Heng:** Cloud Knights? How is that possible...?
*(Wave 3: Disciples of Sanctus Medicus: Shape Shifter*2; Cloud Knights Patroller*2)*
**Dan Heng:** It's endless... Has the memoria tide returned?
**???: ** The fires of abomination reignite...

### Part 207 — `databank/missions/chapter-07-back-to-earth.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 6 — Earth, Bear the Suffering of All > Talk with the Mysterious Mountain Dweller


**???: ** How could I be so easily forsaken?
**Dan Heng:** Dan... Feng?
**Dan Heng:** Is this another Memosnatcher trick?
**"Dan Feng":** Vidyadhara are supposed to forget the past during hatching rebirth, but the Preceptors interfered back then and allowed "me" to remain deep in your consciousness.
**"Dan Feng":** If you do not wish to use that bloodstained name, you may call me "Obliviated Memory."
**Dan Heng:** ...That's not necessary. Though we've long parted ways, I won't ever forget you.
**"Dan Feng":** So, who is that giant?
**Dan Heng:** That is Amphoreus's "Earth" demigod. He should be on our side, but he was led astray and intends to seize the power of dragon transmutation.
**"Dan Feng":** The dragon descends upon the earth, and all beings look to it in reverence. You and I know that all life in the universe covets Permanence.
**Dan Heng:** Are you trying to say I'll end up like you?
**"Dan Feng":** One day you will, and as I see it, that day won't be far from the end of this journey.
**Dan Heng:** Let's move on to another topic. I have no intention of passing judgment on "Dan Feng," and I have even less desire to become entangled with him again.
**Dan Heng:** I only have one concern right now: bringing my companions back safely.
**Dan Heng:** You can either help me deal with these abominations or return to the shadows of memory, Dan Feng.
**"Dan Feng":** Alright. The filthy remnants of the memoria tide still run rampant. Let me accompany you through these ruins. You could use the help.
**"Dan Feng":** And presumably, that giant who craves the power of dragons won't just let things rest.
**Dan Heng:** Do as you please. I'm moving ahead.

### Part 233 — `databank/missions/chapter-07-back-to-earth.md`

**Context:** Complete Dialogue > Mission 6 — Earth, Bear the Suffering of All > Talk with Terravox, the Earth Demigod


**"Hysilens":** In the battle against Georios, the Imperator could not surmount this mountain and thus had to break it from the inside. Terravox's betrayal was key to the Flame-Chase Army's victory.
**Terravox:** The age of the gods was coming to an end. Living beings had adapted to the changes amidst the rifts of the earth, and now they had to learn to survive in the crevices of the epics.
**Terravox:** Someone had to watch over the "Earth." So I agreed to a deal. I would take the Titan's divine authority and sink into the cliffs, using my flesh and blood forged of stone to mend the land. But...
**Terravox:** There was one "betrayal" I could never forget.
**Terravox:** The "Mountainbreaker"... refused to believe that was my choice, even till his dying breath. Faced with having to surrender to the Imperator, he chose to die instead.
**"Dan Feng":** So this is why you decided to conspire with "Evernight."
**Terravox:** "Time's" strange visitor appeared before me. The great power from beyond the sky called the Remembrance... It's incredible.
**Terravox:** Geocles was able to be reborn as a "memosprite." He mumbled about the times we fought side by side, just like how all the lives this body carries speak to me.
**Terravox:** "Evernight" did not ask for much in return. She only wished for me to use the divine authority of "Earth" to hide her tracks.
**"Hysilens":** Since you're being so candid, does this mean... you've given up resisting?
**Terravox:** Hah, resistance...
**Terravox:** For a beast on the brink of death, what is the point of resistance?
**"Hysilens":** I see... A thousand years have passed, and your mind has long since been shattered.
**Terravox:** This frail body bears the weight of all those I've betrayed, and now, my only wish is to rest with them...
**Terravox:** In the unchanging past.
**Dan Heng:** ...
**Terravox:** Go, Long's Scion. That fiery blaze has already burned through "Earth's" protection, revealing the secrets underneath...
**Terravox:** The deepest depths in Amphoreus. The great tomb where all the past is buried.
**Terravox:** The one you seek, "Evernight," walked out of that place. Before her arrival, no one knew of its existence.
**"Dan Feng":** In other words, that is the place where "Evernight" came into being...
**"Hysilens":** And it is also where she is now.
**Dan Heng:** ...
**Dan Heng:** You sank willingly into the past, and now your "memories" have turned into this form... Was it worth it?
**Terravox:** At least I still remember.
**Terravox:** As long as I never forget, they will live on.
**Dan Heng:** Primitive and animalistic... I understand these instincts.
**Dan Heng:** Have you ever regretted anything?
**Terravox:** The earth is not a lonely island in the middle of the ocean. It is the sum of all the living things that stand on it.
**Terravox:** I have never regretted defending the creatures of this world.
**Dan Heng:** Then, a final question...
